# Sadar Bersuara Non-Functional Audit

Security, compliance, performance, reliability, supply chain and privacy audit of the backend, 21 October 2026. Every row names its evidence: a repo path with a line, a test name, or a command with its output. A claim without evidence is a gap. Controls already described elsewhere are linked, not repeated: [Security Controls](architecture.md#security-controls) and [Performance Budgets](architecture.md#performance-budgets) in docs/architecture.md, engineering choices in [decisions.md](decisions.md), procedures in [runbook.md](runbook.md).

## Scope and Method

- **Date and base:** 21 October 2026, commit `64da220` plus the uncommitted working tree after the audit's fix batches (auth-session-access, input-files-api, data-logging-privacy, ops-perf-supply, e2e-api). Migrations 0029 to 0034 were added by those batches.
- **Scope:** backend only: `server/`, `worker/`, `db/`, `shared/`, server-side `app/**/*.server.ts` modules, `infra/`, `.github/`, docs. The browser UI under `app/` belongs to the frontend owner; requirements that only the UI can meet have status `frontend` and are listed in [Frontend Handoff](#frontend-handoff).
- **Target:** OWASP ASVS 5.0 Level 2 (PLAN §10.5 "Target OWASP ASVS 5.0 Level 2"). Level 3 rows are judged too; a Level 3 gap is "beyond target" and is a recommendation, not a required fix.
- **Requirement text** comes only from these copies, checked against the upstream files on 21 October 2026:

| Standard | Version | Source | Local Check |
|---|---|---|---|
| OWASP Application Security Verification Standard | 5.0.0 (345 requirements: 70 L1, 183 L2, 92 L3) | https://github.com/OWASP/ASVS/releases/tag/v5.0.0_release, asset `OWASP_Application_Security_Verification_Standard_5.0.0_en.csv` | sha256 `6124dba176dc563f66363a11ae0c47f9b86b8a4a84c66a793670bd196ed86cd5`, equal to the release asset |
| OWASP Top 10 | 2025 | https://github.com/OWASP/Top10/tree/master/2025/docs/en (`A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control.md` to `A10_2025-Mishandling_of_Exceptional_Conditions.md`) | all 10 git blob hashes equal to the upstream files |
| OWASP API Security Top 10 | 2023 | https://github.com/OWASP/API-Security/tree/master/editions/2023/en (`0xa1-broken-object-level-authorization.md` to `0xaa-unsafe-consumption-of-apis.md`) | all 10 git blob hashes equal to the upstream files |
| UU 27/2022 (PDP), Permenkominfo 5/2020, PP 17/2025 | as cited in PLAN §11 and the critique table | articles cited in PLAN.md, or fetched from pasal.id with the URL and quoted sentence in the citing row or in docs/runbook.md (Pasal 7, 9 and 46 are quoted in the runbook, Pasal 16, 22, 43 and 65 only in their rows here) | PP 33/2026 comes from an unofficial copy: every claim on it is marked "source unofficial, user to confirm" |

- **Statuses:** `pass` (evidence shows the requirement met), `gap` (not met; severity given), `na` (does not apply, reason given), `frontend` (only the browser UI can meet it), `decision` (waiting on a user, team, legal or repository-admin decision; no code fix applies until it is made).
- **Severity:** CVSS 4.0 qualitative rating (Critical, High, Medium, Low) with the base-vector reasoning in the row. Organisational and legal obligations get no CVSS score; their severity is "decision".
- **How rows were produced:** one auditor per area wrote rows against the requirement text, a second agent tried to refute every gap (refuted gaps became `pass` or `na` with the refutation as evidence: V12.3.4, A04-CWE-319/757, REL-08, REL-12), then every confirmed gap at L1 or L2 and every confirmed backend gap outside ASVS was fixed test-first or turned into a recorded decision. Fixed rows show `pass` with the new code and test names.
- **Line numbers** are from 21 October 2026. The 30-row re-check sample (marked "Re-checked against the tree") and the open gap rows (status `gap` or `decision` in ASVS, Top 10 and API) were re-read after the last fix batch. Review round 2 (21 October 2026) re-read every `server/auth.server.ts`, `server/auth.test.ts`, `server/app.ts` and `server/app.test.ts` line they cite against the tree after the last auth change and its own app changes, so those cite current lines; their citations of other files are from the re-check, and a few cite documentation lines by row name instead. Rows rewritten by a fix batch cite the lines of that batch; rows the fixes did not touch keep the lines recorded at audit time earlier the same day. Where code moved since, the named symbol or test is the stable reference. Probe scripts under `/tmp` were throwaway and are not kept; their printed output is quoted in the row.
- **Live runs** used a production build on 127.0.0.1:5198 or 5199 against disposable copies of the dev database, dropped afterwards. One audit test run wrote to the dev database by mistake; it is recorded under [Open Decisions](#open-decisions).

V6.5.5, V12.1.2, V12.3.1 and V12.3.3 are accepted deviations, decided on 22 October 2026 (docs/decisions.md); the others are operator launch steps or open team decisions, and none has a code fix waiting except the optional GlitchTip first-account check under V13.2.3. `bun test infra/nfr-audit.test.ts` recomputes every count in this section from the tables. Every other open ASVS gap is Level 3, beyond target.

Highest severities still open outside ASVS: A06 (Medium), A06-CWE-311/312/313/316/256/522 (Medium), API2 (Medium), SC-14 Main branch protection and separation of duties (Medium). All of them are documented deferrals or decisions listed in [Open Decisions](#open-decisions).

## ASVS 5.0 Matrix

One table per chapter, every requirement once, in the standard's order. Requirement text: the ASVS 5.0.0 CSV named in [Scope and Method](#scope-and-method).

No runtime template engine in package.json:30-51, user input is never used as a template. |

Logs pass one pre-serialised JSON string to console (server/log.ts:8-9 writeLog, :77-79 writeError), so printf directives in user text are inert: `bun -e 'console.log(JSON.stringify({m:"%s %d %o %c x"}))'` printed '{"m":"%s %d %o %c x"}'. Message formatting uses compiled Paraglide functions with named parameters (server/emails/render.ts); no util.format with a user-supplied format string. |
| V1.3.11 | L2 | pass | No SMTP/IMAP from the app: mail goes as JSON over HTTPS to Brevo, SES v2 or Mailpit (server/email.ts:86-180 exchange/post, :226-260 SES), so the app assembles no raw headers and CR/LF in a value cannot add a header. Subjects (full grep of server/emails/render.ts) take only codes, counts and message constants (e.g. :235, :267-271, :315, :452, :490), except the invite subject m.mail_invite_subject({operator}) at :594 which carries the tenant name set by staff, still sent as a JSON string field. SES From display name RFC 2047 encoded or quoted (server/email.ts:206-222); recipient is the account address. Tests server/email.test.ts (bun test: pass). |
| V1.3.12 | L3 | gap | **Gap, beyond target (Low):** One failing request with a 16 KB path (Bun's header cap) blocks the event loop for tens of milliseconds while its error is scrubbed for the reporter, and a long error message that quotes user text costs the same on any error. CVSS 4.0 AV:N/AC:L/AT:P/PR:N/UI:N/VC:N/VI:N/VA:L: needs an error path, small availability impact. Evidence: server/scrub.ts:5 `EMAIL_RE = /[\w.+-]+@[\w-]+(?:\.[\w-]+)+/g` has no left anchor, and `scrub` (server/scrub.ts:22-32) runs every replace before `.slice(0, max)`, so the whole input is scanned before it is cut. Callers: the GlitchTip event builder (server/error-report.ts:122, the request path among the fields, only when ERROR_REPORTING is on) and, since the fix batches, every local error record (server/log.ts:128-129, message capped at 512 after scanning, stack uncapped). Timing of the current scrub on '/'+'a'\*n with Bun 1.4.3: `bun -e` printed "8000 16.1 ms" and "16000 67.1 ms" (quadratic growth); the verifier measured 2090.9 ms at 64000 characters. Fix or recommendation: Small and safe: cut the input before the replaces (`text.slice(0, max)` first) and anchor EMAIL_RE with a lookbehind (`(?<![\w.+-])`); the verifier timed the anchored pattern at 0.6 ms on 100,000 characters. |
| V1.4.1 | L2 | pass | Application code is memory-safe TypeScript on Bun (bounds-checked Buffer/TypedArray). Unmanaged decoders (ffmpeg, poppler, libheif, qpdf, libvips via sharp) run with pixel caps and timeouts (worker/evidence-common.ts:51,61; worker/heic.ts:33-39; app/features/campus/settings.server.ts:195-199), in child processes with output caps and SIGKILL timeout (worker/tools.ts:13-62), inside the worker-media container: read_only, cap_drop ALL, no-new-privileges, mem_limit 1536m, internal networks (infra/compose.yaml:21-26 x-hardened, :250-294). |
| V1.4.2 | L2 | pass | Numeric input is range-checked below 2^53: Upload-Offset /^\d{1,15}$/ (server/api/upload.server.ts:58,718-721), evidence size zod int().positive() and &lt;= EVIDENCE_LIMITS (shared/report-schema.ts evidenceFile size, superRefine 'error_evidence_too_large'), DB CHECK evidence_declared_size_check &gt; 0 (db/schema/reports.ts:267), page counts capped MAX_PDF_PAGES (worker/pdf.ts:9,73), pixels compared to MAX_FULL_DECODE_PIXELS (worker/heic.ts:37), cursor page Number.isInteger and &gt;= 2 (app/features/issues/issues.server.ts:328). |
| V1.4.3 | L2 | pass | Garbage-collected runtime, no manual pointers. Resources are released in finally blocks: scratch dirs (worker/tools.ts:67-76), timers (worker/tools.ts:49-53), killed children (worker/tools.ts:45-48), boot sweep of leftovers (worker/tools.ts:79-87). Tests worker/tools.test.ts:53 'removes its directory after use and on error', :71 'boot removes dirs a killed job left', :21 'kills a child past its timeout' (bun test: pass). |
| V1.5.1 | L1 | pass | No XML parser processes untrusted input: no XML parsing dependency (bun.lock has no fast-xml-parser, xml2js, sax or xmldom; only yaml and protobufjs, not used on user input); SVG/XML uploads are refused before any decoder (shared/evidence.ts:9-20, worker/evidence-common.ts:90-97, shared/campus.ts:129-143, tests worker/evidence.test.ts:235, shared/campus.test.ts:121). ### V3 Web Frontend Security

| V3.1.1 | L3 | gap | **Gap, beyond target (Low):** No statement of required browser security features or behaviour when absent. L3, beyond target. Evidence: docs/architecture.md:855-857 (Security Controls rows CSRF, CSP and XSS, Other headers) and docs/decisions.md:115 (TLS row) list what the server sends: HTTPS, HSTS, CSP, nosniff, Referrer-Policy. No doc states which browser features are required or what the app does when they are missing (grep -i 'browser support\|unsupported browser' docs/\*.md PLAN.md: no hit); only the no-JS notice on the report form is documented. Fix or recommendation: Add a 'Browser Requirements' paragraph to docs/architecture.md Security Controls: required (TLS 1.2+, CSP Level 3 with strict-dynamic and hashes, SameSite cookies, Fetch Metadata or Origin on POST); behaviour when missing (POST without Origin or Sec-Fetch-Site same-origin is refused 403 by server/middleware/csrf.ts, report form needs JS and shows the noscript notice). CVSS 4.0 n/a doc-only, rated Low. |

Implemented as documented (docs/architecture.md Rate limiter and Code bombing rows, including malicious lockout): Turnstile on every code request, failing closed (server/auth.server.ts:631, server/turnstile.server.ts); Better Auth limiter (server/auth.server.ts:136, :547-551); 3 attempts per code with an atomic consume (server/account-otp.server.ts:258-274); per address and client 60 s and 8 a day plus 20 a day per address (server/email-log.ts:44-46); WhatsApp per number and client plus 20 a day per number (shared/whatsapp.ts:38-41); TOTP 5 attempts per challenge and the account lockout on the sign-in challenge, the step-up's 5-failure sign-out on /masuk/konfirmasi, and Better Auth's per-IP /two-factor/ limit on direct HTTP calls only (V6.1.3). Tests: server/auth.test.ts:245 "needs a Turnstile token", :747 "three wrong codes end the code; a code works once", :1611 "one address waits 60 s and gets 8 codes a day", :1641 "other clients never lock the owner out", :1710 "a flood from one address is throttled"; app/features/moderation/step-up.test.ts:90 "five wrong codes sign the session out". |

| V6.3.5 | L3 | gap | **Gap, beyond target (Low):** Users are not told about a passed code step with a failed TOTP, many failed codes, or a new client. CVSS 4.0 AV:N/AC:L/AT:P/PR:N/UI:N/VC:L/VI:N/VA:N. Evidence: No code sends a notice on suspicious or partial authentication: messages/id.json has no mail\_\* key for sign-in or security events (only complaint, deadline, demand, invite, issue, reply, report, survey, track, unfollow keys). Fix or recommendation: Recommendation: email or WhatsApp notice (mail_security\_\* keys) after 3 failed codes on one address, after a code step whose TOTP challenge was never completed, and after a staff sign-in from a new /64. Respect the email ceiling. |
| V6.3.6 | L3 | gap | **Gap, beyond target (Low):** Email is the only factor for citizen accounts that sign in by email. CVSS 4.0 AV:N/AC:L/AT:P/PR:N/UI:N/VC:H/VI:L/VA:N for that one account after mailbox compromise. Evidence: Email codes are a full sign-in factor for citizens: send by channel (server/account-otp.server.ts:368), verify and sign in (:423-464, `signedIn` at :276). For staff, PLAN.md §10.5 says email and WhatsApp do not count as a factor and TOTP is mandatory: the two-factor after hook is re-attached to the account verify path (server/auth.server.ts:268 accountOtpStepUp). Citizen single-factor sign-in is a recorded decision for L2 (docs/decisions.md "Citizen sign-in strength (ASVS V6.3.3, V6.6.1)"). Fix or recommendation: Recommendation: keep it for citizens at L2 under the V6.3.3 decision. At L3, require TOTP or a passkey (P5) as the factor, with email used only for recovery and notices. |
| V6.3.7 | L3 | gap | **Gap, beyond target (Low):** An account whose unproven email or number moves to another account through the release path is not told. CVSS 4.0 AV:N/AC:L/AT:N/PR:N/UI:N/VC:N/VI:L/VA:N. Evidence: No notice on authentication-detail changes: TOTP enrolment (/two-factor/enable), a number added (app/features/report/add-phone.server.ts) and an unproven identifier released to another account (server/account-otp.server.ts:443-444, `releasedEmail` or `phoneNumber: null`) all send nothing; messages/id.json has no security mail key. Since the fix pass the backend can list and end a user's other sessions (V7.4.3, V7.5.2), but nothing prompts the user after such a change. Fix or recommendation: Recommendation: send a notice to the remaining proven channel on release, on TOTP enrolment and on a number change (mail_security\_\* keys, sendLogged), and add tests. |
| V6.3.8 | L3 | gap | **Gap, beyond target (Low):** And an accepted exposure recorded as an open question. Registered emails and numbers can be enumerated at 20 requests per 60 s per IP; lookup has no Turnstile. CVSS 4.0 AV:N/AC:L/AT:N/PR:N/UI:N/VC:L/VI:N/VA:N. Evidence: Account existence is observable: /account/lookup returns found true or false, and `unproven` for an identifier held unproven (server/account-otp.server.ts:302-314); send-code for an unknown account returns 404 ACCOUNT_NOT_FOUND (:229, test server/auth.test.ts "an unknown account gets no code"); check-new returns `taken` (:162-210). Documented as an accepted exposure in docs/architecture.md (Rate limiter row, Open Question 9) and ADR-08. The code send is not awaited, against timing attacks (server/auth.server.ts:169 "Caps first, send not awaited"). Fix or recommendation: Recommendation (product decision, Open Question 9): put Turnstile on /account/lookup and /account/check-new, or give one uniform answer ("if an account exists a code was sent") and resolve the channel on the server. |
| V6.4.1 | L1 | pass | Activation secrets are sign-in codes only: generateRandomString(6, "0-9") (server/account-otp.server.ts:346) uses crypto.getRandomValues with rejection sampling (node_modules/@better-auth/utils/dist/random.mjs:33-47), expires after OTP_TTL_SECONDS 300 (shared/whatsapp.ts:55, server/account-otp.server.ts:354), and is consumed once (server/account-otp.server.ts:254-270). There are no passwords, so a code can never become a long-term secret. Staff invitations expire after 7 days (shared/campus.ts:2), are single use, and need a signed-in user with the same proven email (app/features/campus/members.server.ts:419-430). Tests "three wrong codes end the code; a code works once", "a pre-registered email cannot take over its owner". |

| V6.5.5 | L2 | decision | **Decision, accepted 22 October 2026 (Low):** a TOTP code is accepted during its own 30 s step and the previous one, so for up to 60 s against the requirement's 30 s maximum. Accepted as a recorded deviation under the user's delegation of 22 October 2026 (docs/decisions.md Security row "TOTP acceptance window (ASVS V6.5.5)"): RFC 6238 §5.2 says "We RECOMMEND that at most one time step is allowed as the network delay" (https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc6238.txt), and accepting the current step only would fail a code typed across a step boundary. Evidence: claimTotpStep (server/auth.server.ts:454, called for the TOTP verify path at :648, the next-step refusal at :474) refuses a code from the next step (`currentStep` in server/totp-step.ts), so acceptance went from 90 s to 60 s; each step is claimed once, so a code cannot be replayed inside the window; test server/auth.test.ts "a TOTP code works once; a backup code signs in" (future step 401 INVALID_CODE, replay 401). Sign-in codes live 300 s. Also recorded in docs/architecture.md "TOTP replay and lifetime". Revisit: if staff authenticators show no skew failures, or ASVS changes the maximum. |
| V6.5.6 | L3 | gap | **Gap, beyond target (Low):** Narrowed. A stolen phone that holds a proven WhatsApp sign-in number cannot be detached from the account: outside the sign-up release path (server/account-otp.server.ts:444) no backend function or CLI sets users.phone_number to null, so the only operator remedy is deleting the account (db:subject delete). Revocation is operator-run only, with no self-service. Evidence: TOTP and backup codes can now be revoked: db/totp-reset.ts (`bun run db:totp-reset <email>`) deletes the two_factors row (secret and backup codes), clears two_factor_enabled, bumps users.updated_at (older sessions fail the staff check), deletes the person's sessions and writes audit_log `member.totp_reset` per campus; docs/runbook.md:176 "Lost Authenticator"; tests db/totp-reset.test.ts "lost authenticator: factor, sessions gone, audited", "only staff with a factor". Sessions can be ended per person or for everyone with db/sessions.ts (V7.4.5). Fix or recommendation: Recommendation: an operator command that clears a proven number (audited, sessions ended), and optionally a self-service revoke behind step-up. |

A new send deletes the earlier code (server/account-otp.server.ts:349-351), so a previous code stops working. A code is consumed once (254-270). Tests "a code proves only the number it went to" (INVALID_OTP after the destination changed), "three wrong codes end the code; a code works once". |
| V6.6.3 | L2 | pass | Rate limiting bounds guesses on the 6-digit sign-in codes. Per code: 3 attempts (server/account-otp.server.ts:37 ALLOWED_ATTEMPTS, checked in consumeCode :258-274) and a 300 s life (OTP_TTL_SECONDS, shared/whatsapp.ts:57). Per destination: at most 20 sign-in codes a day per address or number from all clients, with no spacing across clients (server/email-log.ts:46 ADDRESS_LIMITS, checked first in reserveOtp :184; shared/whatsapp.ts:41 perNumberDayAllClients), and per (address, client) 60 s spacing and 8 a day (server/email-log.ts:44 OTP_LIMITS, :185). Per client: the Better Auth limiter, 20 per 60 s on verify-code (server/auth.server.ts:136 CGNAT_LIMIT, :551). Budget: an attacker spread over at least 3 clients gets 20 codes x 3 attempts = 60 guesses a day per channel against 10^6 codes, and 120 a day for an account reachable by email and WhatsApp (separate counters), about 1.2 x 10^-4 a day; the budget is stated in docs/architecture.md Multi-Host Auth 'Rate limiter' row (V6.1.1). The 64-bit code ASVS suggests is not used (6 digits, about 20 bits); the rate limits carry the control. Tests: server/auth.test.ts:747 "three wrong codes end the code; a code works once", :1710 "a flood from one address is throttled" (40 verify calls end in 429), :1611 "one address waits 60 s and gets 8 codes a day", :1654 "one proven address gets 20 codes a day" (20th send 200, 21st 429); server/whatsapp.test.ts:150 "one number gets 20 sign-in codes a day". (Corrected in review round 2, 21 October 2026: the row said 8 codes a day per address and 24 guesses, which the cross-client ceiling of the V6.1.1 change no longer gives.) |

| V8.2.3 | L2 | pass | Writes: parsers build explicit objects, never spread raw form data (shared/replies.ts checkReply returns {agency, senderRole, body, sourceUrl}; app/features/demands/rating-form.ts:24 returns {score, showName}; app/features/demands/demand-form.ts:96 fields object; app/features/campus/campus-actions.server.ts:48-50 FIELDS allowlist); Better Auth user fields not client-settable (auth.test.ts 'user fields cannot be set directly'); request body allowlist per auth path (server/auth.server.ts:81-98); DB column grants: sb_app cannot UPDATE users.superadmin (db/superadmin.test.ts 'the app role cannot set the flag'), sb_media writes only output columns (db/tenant.test.ts 'writes outputs, never the admin decision', 'reads evidence only, never reports or contacts'). Reads: report_contacts restrictive policy admin-only (db/tenant.test.ts 'reporter inserts but cannot read back; admin can'); tracking never reads report_contacts (tracking.server.ts:52); DTO mapping instead of rows (app/features/whatsapp/admin.server.ts:46-62); grep for .select() in app/server/shared non-test code finds only national_aggregates, dashboard_snapshots, whatsapp_sender (mapped to DTO) and forward.server.ts internal copy; public API keys checked (server/api/public-data.test.ts:87 PII_KEYS, piiIn asserted at :356, :401, :453, :471, :508). |
| V8.2.4 | L3 | gap | **Gap, beyond target (Low):** No adaptive authentication or authorization based on location, device or IP change, at sign-in or during a session (beyond L2 target). Evidence: Only fixed contextual rules exist: Turnstile on code requests, the per-client limiter (server/auth.server.ts:136 CGNAT_LIMIT), the 12 h staff cap (:128 STAFF_SESSION_MS), 30 idle minutes for TOTP accounts and 30 days overall (V7.3.1, V7.3.2), and the 300 s step-up (app/features/moderation/step-up.server.ts:14 FRESH_MS). No location, device or time-of-day signal is evaluated at session start, and nothing re-evaluates a session when its IP or user agent changes: the root middleware (server/request-context.server.ts) reads only the session, the campus role and the superadmin flag; sessions.ip_address and user_agent are stored but read by no decision. Fix or recommendation: Recommendation (L3, beyond target): store sessions.ip_address and user_agent hash at creation (Better Auth already records them) and, for /kelola and /admin, require the step-up (requireFresh) when the request's client IP /24 prefix or user agent differs from the session's; document the rule. CVSS 4.0 rationale: AV:N/AC:H/AT:P/PR:H/UI:N, needs a stolen staff session first, low added impact. |
| V8.3.1 | L1 | pass | Re-checked against the tree on 21 October 2026. All authorization runs on the server: the React Router root middleware (server/request-context.server.ts), requireAdmin and requireSuperadmin in \*.server.ts loaders and actions, and Postgres FORCE RLS as the last layer (db/tenant.server.ts:75 set_config of app.org_id, app.member_role and app.request_id inside the transaction). The tenant comes from Host only (server/request-context.server.ts:96), never from the session's activeOrganizationId, which CI greps for (.github/workflows/ci.yml:81 "No activeOrganizationId reads"). Role and flags are read from the database per request. |
| V8.3.2 | L3 | pass | Sessions are database-backed with no cookie cache: grep -rn cookieCache server returns nothing (Better Auth default off), session block server/auth.server.ts:366-370 has no cookieCache; superadmin flag and membership role are read from the DB on every gated request (server/request-context.server.ts:125, :140-142); member removal sets role reporter and deletes every session of the user in the same tx (app/features/campus/members.server.ts:509 and :516); superadmin grant or revoke bumps users.updated_at (db/superadmin.ts:10) and verifiedSession refuses sessions created before updated_at (server/auth.server.ts:592-597); tests request-context.test.ts 'no session, or a stale one, fails closed', auth.test.ts 'staff role, TOTP and a session after the last change'. |
| V8.3.3 | L3 | na | No delegated service-to-service call carries a consumer token: the web process decides with the consumer's own session and role, and enqueues jobs in the same tenant transaction (db/tenant.test.ts 'a job commits and rolls back with the tenant transaction'); workers run system jobs as sb_app or sb_media with ID-only payloads (docs/architecture.md:220) and re-check object state before acting (worker/evidence-publish.ts:58 issue still published, worker/email-send.ts:324 unpublished skipped, :612-622 recipient still a member). |
| V8.4.1 | L2 | pass | FORCE RLS on all 28 tenant tables (psql: 'forced\|enabled 28\|28'; db/fresh-migrate.test.ts:48 expects 28); catalog test db/tenant.test.ts 'every table the app roles reach has FORCE RLS or is allowlisted', 'app roles cannot bypass RLS or own tables'; isolation as sb_app: 'reads only the current org', 'refuses a write into another org', 'cannot update or delete another org's rows', 'child rows cannot point at another org's report', 'a pooled connection fails loudly after a tenant transaction'; Host-bound membership: request-context.test.ts 'a member of another campus is 403', campus-routes.test.ts 'an admin of campus A is refused on campus B'; public API server/api/public-data.test.ts 'unpublished and other tenants' issues are 404'; upload.test.ts 'another tenant cannot see the row'. Controlled cross-tenant paths, each audited: national_aggregates (NOLOGIN BYPASSRLS owner sb_aggregates, suppressed counts, architecture.md:221), forwardReport (admin role checked in the source tenant; forward.test.ts 'a admin of the other campus cannot forward it'), superadmin resendFirstInvitation (platform function, flag re-checked in tx, campuses.server.ts:230 and :250). Tests passed in the 144-test and 181-test runs above. |
| V8.4.2 | L3 | gap | **Gap, beyond target (Low):** No device posture check and no contextual risk analysis for administrative interfaces (beyond L2 target). Evidence: Layers present for /kelola and /admin: TOTP on every sign-in with trusted devices refused (server/auth.server.ts:618-627 `trustDevice` field refused), the staff checks in verifiedSession (:843: TOTP, session newer than users.updated_at, 12 h cap), 30 idle minutes for TOTP accounts, the 300 s TOTP step-up for member, settings, campus and sender changes with the session ended after 5 failures (app/features/moderation/step-up.server.ts:14-15, :38 requireFresh), sessions deleted on removal (app/features/campus/members.server.ts:516), Cloudflare edge plus Turnstile; GlitchTip and Umami UIs only from the ops network with their own logins. Missing: device security posture assessment and contextual risk analysis. Fix or recommendation: Recommendation (L3, beyond target): put /kelola and /admin behind Cloudflare Access with device posture rules (WARP client, OS version) in front of the app's TOTP, or bind staff sessions to client IP prefix and user agent with step-up on change (see V8.2.4); record the choice in docs/decisions.md. CVSS 4.0 rationale: AV:N/AC:H/AT:P/PR:H/UI:N, exploitation needs stolen staff credentials and TOTP or session, low added impact. |

### V9 Self-contained Tokens

| ID | Level | Status | Evidence or Gap |
|---|---|---|---|
| V9.1.1 | L1 | pass | Re-checked against the tree on 21 October 2026. Signed links (lacak, berhenti, berhenti-tuntutan, survei): HMAC-SHA256 over version.body (server/signed-link.ts:63), compared with timingSafeEqual (:93) inside verifyLink (:80) before the body is used. WhatsApp job payloads: AES-256-GCM with an auth tag (server/seal.ts:23, :37). Better Auth session cookies are HMAC-signed references to database rows with cookieCache off. Tests: server/signed-link.test.ts:85 "any tampered character is refused", server/seal.test.ts "other purpose, key or tampering fails", worker/whatsapp-send.test.ts "a tampered payload is dropped". |
| V9.1.2 | L1 | pass | No token carries an algorithm field, so there is nothing to negotiate and no 'none': signed links hardcode createHmac("sha256") (server/signed-link.ts:63), token format is version.body.sig with exactly three parts (server/signed-link.ts:86-87); job and creds seals hardcode "aes-256-gcm" (server/seal.ts:23,37); Better Auth cookie signing hardcodes HMAC SHA-256 (node_modules/better-call/dist/crypto.mjs:4-5). Symmetric only, no asymmetric path, so no key confusion. No JWT library in use (OAuth/JWT grep: no files). |
| V9.1.3 | L1 | pass | Re-checked against the tree on 21 October 2026. Keys come only from the deployment environment: LACAK_LINK_KEYS keyring with at least 32 bytes per version (server/signed-link.ts:24 MIN_KEY_BYTES, :40-41), WHATSAPP_JOB_KEY exactly 32 bytes (server/seal.ts:13), wired in infra/compose.yaml:113, :118, :179, :243. The token's version prefix only selects a preconfigured key; there is no jku, x5u or jwk-style header. Tests: server/signed-link.test.ts:36 "refuses empty, malformed and short keys", :110 "an old key still verifies until it is dropped". |
| V9.2.1 | L1 | pass | Signed links carry an expiry 30 days after issue (server/signed-link.ts:4,58-60,69) and verifyLink refuses at or after it (server/signed-link.ts:105); test 'valid for 30 days, then expired' (server/signed-link.test.ts:56). WhatsApp job payload carries expiresAt = OTP_TTL (server/whatsapp.server.ts:167-171), worker refuses a missing or non-numeric expiresAt (worker/whatsapp-send.ts:30-36) and drops expired jobs (worker/whatsapp-send.ts:76-77); test 'an expired code is dropped, never sent' (worker/whatsapp-send.test.ts:99). Observation, not exploitable: verifyLink uses Number(seconds) so a MAC-valid body with a missing or non-numeric expiry field verifies forever; `bun /tmp/v9v10/nan.ts` printed '"" true', '"\|abc" true', '"\|0" false'. Only a key holder can mint such a body (signLink always writes an integer, server/signed-link.ts:69-72), and a key holder can already mint expiry 9999999999, so it grants nothing beyond key possession; inconsistent with the fail-closed whatsapp-send path. |
| V9.2.2 | L2 | pass | Signed links bind purpose (lacak \| berhenti \| berhenti-tuntutan \| survei, server/signed-link.ts:6-11) into the MAC'd body (:71) and verifyLink requires the caller's expected purpose (:103); each route passes its own purpose (app/routes/account/track.tsx:48 'lacak', app/routes/issues/unfollow.tsx:38 'berhenti', app/routes/demands/unfollow.tsx:32 'berhenti-tuntutan', app/features/issues/survey.server.ts:42-44 'survei'). Seals bind purpose as GCM AAD ('job' vs 'creds', server/seal.ts:7,24,38) and use separate keys (WHATSAPP_JOB_KEY vs WHATSAPP_CREDS_KEY, worker/whatsapp/runtime.ts:35,46). Tests: 'another tenant or purpose is refused' (server/signed-link.test.ts:67), 'other purpose, key or tampering fails' (server/seal.test.ts:17). |
| V9.2.3 | L2 | pass | Audience for signed links is the tenant: orgId is inside the MAC'd body (server/signed-link.ts:71) and verifyLink rejects unless it equals the request's tenant id (server/signed-link.ts:103); callers pass tenant.id resolved from the Host (app/features/report/signed-link.server.ts:16-19, app/routes/account/track.tsx:48, app/features/issues/survey.server.ts:43). Job seals are consumed only by the whatsapp-send worker with its own key (worker/whatsapp-send.ts:11,29). Test 'another tenant or purpose is refused' (server/signed-link.test.ts:67) uses OTHER_ORG and gets null. |
| V9.2.4 | L2 | pass | One LACAK_LINK_KEYS keyring signs links for every tenant (single env value, infra/compose.yaml:100,161), which is the V9.2.4 case; every token carries orgId as an audience restriction under the MAC (server/signed-link.ts:15-21,71) and verification enforces it (:103). Audiences are not dynamically provisioned by clients: orgId is set by the issuer from the DB row in worker/email-send.ts:175-181. Test 'another tenant or purpose is refused' (server/signed-link.test.ts:67). |

### V11 Cryptography

| ID | Level | Status | Evidence or Gap |
|---|---|---|---|
| V11.1.1 | L2 | pass | docs/architecture.md 'Key Management' section: policy with NIST SP 800-57 Part 1 Rev. 5 section 7 key states (fetched PDF, [S143]), per-key generation, checks, holders, rotation and retirement; BETTER_AUTH_SECRETS lifecycle corrected (new version, db:reseal-two-factors, then drop) in the offboarding and Keys rows; docs/runbook.md 'Key Holders' list definition. AUDIT_HMAC_KEY three-container holding recorded as a deviation. |
| V11.1.2 | L2 | pass | docs/architecture.md 'Key Management' inventory table: every key and algorithm (Better Auth HMAC and XChaCha20-Poly1305, LACAK_LINK_KEYS, AUDIT_HMAC_KEY, WHATSAPP_JOB_KEY, WHATSAPP_CREDS_KEY with its HMAC subkey and the Baileys libsignal keys, OTP SHA-256, TOTP, pgBackRest, restic, age, LUKS, API credentials, database passwords, integrity hashes, TLS) with may-protect and must-not-be-used-for columns. |
| V11.1.3 | L3 | gap | **Gap, beyond target (Low):** No automated crypto discovery that keeps the inventory current; tooling.test.ts has no allowlist of modules allowed to import node:crypto or call getRandomValues/Bun.CryptoHasher. Evidence: Discovery done manually for this audit: grep -rnE 'createHmac\|createHash\|createCipheriv\|randomBytes\|randomUUID\|getRandomValues\|Math.random\|timingSafeEqual\|crypto.subtle\|AwsV4Signer\|...' over repo (excluding node_modules) found server/seal.ts, server/signed-link.ts, server/email-log.ts, server/account-otp.server.ts, server/totp-step.ts, server/csp.server.ts, server/email.ts, worker/whatsapp/auth-state.ts, worker/evidence-\*.ts, shared/report-id.ts, infra/pgbackrest.conf. Fix or recommendation: Beyond target, recommendation: add a tooling.test.ts case that globs server, worker, shared, db, app \*.ts (non-test) for node:crypto, crypto.subtle, Bun.CryptoHasher, getRandomValues, Math.random and fails on any file not in an allowlist that mirrors the inventory (small and safe). |
| V11.1.4 | L3 | gap | **Gap, beyond target (Low):** No migration plan to post-quantum or newer standards. Symmetric primitives (AES-256-GCM, HMAC-SHA-256, 32-byte keys) are already quantum-tolerant; at-risk asymmetric crypto is outside app code (Cloudflare TLS, libsignal Curve25519 in Baileys, age X25519 recipients for SOPS). Evidence: grep -n -i 'kuantum\|quantum' PLAN.md docs/\*.md: no output. Fix or recommendation: Beyond target, recommendation: add a short 'Crypto Migration' paragraph to the inventory: symmetric parts stay, asymmetric dependencies (Cloudflare edge TLS hybrid key exchange, age post-quantum recipients when stable, WhatsApp protocol owned by Meta) tracked per release, with the review trigger. |
| V11.2.1 | L2 | pass | All crypto via validated libraries, none hand-rolled except HOTP framing: Bun node:crypto backed by BoringSSL (bun -e output: {"boringssl":"41bf9b59c2ebf277a7aa427e1ecad5cc80dd4d4f","openssl":"1.1.0","bun":"1.4.3"}); AES-256-GCM server/seal.ts:23,37; HMAC-SHA-256 server/signed-link.ts:63, server/email-log.ts:30,37; Better Auth uses @noble/ciphers 2.4.0 xchacha20poly1305 (node_modules/better-auth/dist/crypto/index.mjs:7; noble README line 3 'Audited & minimal JS implementation'); SES SigV4 via aws4fetch AwsV4Signer (server/email.ts:1, service 'ses' at :260) independently recomputed in test 'sesSender &gt; signs the exact body with SigV4' (server/email.test.ts:185-211,244); CSPRNG via crypto.getRandomValues / randomBytes. server/totp-step.ts is RFC 4226 framing over node:crypto HMAC with test server/totp-step.test.ts. Command: bun test server/seal.test.ts server/signed-link.test.ts server/email-log.test.ts server/totp-step.test.ts worker/whatsapp/auth-state.test.ts server/email.test.ts server/csp.test.ts shared/report-id.test.ts -&gt; '69 pass 0 fail 6476 expect() calls'. |
| V11.2.2 | L2 | pass | db/reseal-two-factors.ts re-encrypts two_factors.secret and backup_codes under the current BETTER_AUTH_SECRETS version and counts unreadable rows (exit 1 when stale &gt; 0), script db:reseal-two-factors; tests db/reseal-two-factors.test.ts 'reseals old envelopes under the current version', 'a version missing from the keyring stays and counts'; docs/architecture.md offboarding and Keys rows, docs/runbook.md Offboarding. |
| V11.2.3 | L2 | pass | parseAuthSecrets (server/auth-secrets.ts) requires every BETTER_AUTH_SECRETS version to decode to 32 bytes or more, passed as createAuth secrets in authFromEnv (server/auth.server.ts:557), so boot fails on a weak value; tests server/auth-secrets.test.ts 'keeps the original strings, newest first', 'refuses short, retired-short and malformed values'. |
| V11.2.4 | L3 | gap | **Gap, beyond target (Low):** Better Auth backup-code check uses Array.includes string comparison (node_modules/better-auth/dist/plugins/two-factor/backup-codes/index.mjs:40-41 'status: codes.includes(data.code)'), not constant time; server/totp-step.ts:25-28 returns on the first matching step (leaks which of three steps matched, negligible). Practical exploitation is unlikely behind the Better Auth limiter and network jitter. Evidence: Own comparisons are constant time: timingSafeEqual in server/signed-link.ts:93, server/account-otp.server.ts:95-98 (sameHash), server/totp-step.ts:30; Better Auth cookie HMAC verification uses WebCrypto. Fix or recommendation: Beyond target, recommendation: route /two-factor/verify-backup-code through a wrapper that compares each stored code with timingSafeEqual on equal-length buffers, or file upstream; no change needed for totp-step. |
| V11.2.5 | L3 | pass | Fail closed with no oracle: openSealed failures (wrong key, tamper) return null and the job is dropped (worker/whatsapp-send.ts:27-40 'Wrong key or tampered'); sealed store returns null on decrypt failure (worker/whatsapp/auth-state.ts:38-43); verifyLink returns null for any malformed, unknown-version, bad-MAC, wrong purpose/tenant or expired token with MAC checked before parsing (server/signed-link.ts:85-106); GCM tag set before final (server/seal.ts:39-40); key parsers throw at boot (seal.ts:11-13, signed-link.ts:29-46, email-log.ts:13-17). Tests: 'other purpose, key or tampering fails' (server/seal.test.ts:17), 'any tampered character is refused' (server/signed-link.test.ts:85), 'rows are sealed; another key reads nothing' (worker/whatsapp/auth-state.test.ts:86). No CBC decryption is exposed online (pgBackRest CBC only offline restore), so no padding oracle. |
| V11.3.1 | L1 | pass | No ECB and no RSA PKCS#1 v1.5 anywhere in app or infra config: grep for createCipheriv/createDecipheriv finds only 'aes-256-gcm' (server/seal.ts:23,37); Better Auth uses XChaCha20-Poly1305 (node_modules/better-auth/dist/crypto/index.mjs:7,33); backups aes-256-cbc (infra/pgbackrest.conf:7), a chained mode, not ECB; restic AES-256-CTR (restic.readthedocs.io/en/stable/100_references.html: 'encrypted with AES-256 in counter mode and authenticated using Poly1305-AES'). No RSA keys or signatures in the app. |
| V11.3.2 | L1 | pass | Ciphers in use, stated as facts: app-owned AES-256-GCM with 96-bit nonce and 128-bit tag (server/seal.ts:3-5,23); Better Auth XChaCha20-Poly1305 for two_factors.secret and backup codes (node_modules/better-auth/dist/crypto/index.mjs:30-40); pgBackRest AES-256-CBC (infra/pgbackrest.conf:7; pgbackrest.org/configuration.html allowed values 'none' and 'aes-256-cbc - Advanced Encryption Standard with 256 bit key length'); restic AES-256-CTR with Poly1305-AES; TLS by Cloudflare edge and BoringSSL defaults. All app code uses AES-GCM. |
| V11.3.3 | L2 | pass | Limitation recorded in docs/decisions.md Backups row; infra/backup-db.sh runs pgbackrest verify after each backup and records backup_runs kind pgbackrest-verify (migration 0030_backup_verify, db/schema/backups.ts); /healthz/jobs pages when it is older than 26 h (server/health.ts BACKUP_LIMIT_S; tests server/health.test.ts backups describe). verify semantics quoted from pgbackrest.org/command.html. |
| V11.3.4 | L3 | pass | Fresh random 96-bit nonce per seal from randomBytes (server/seal.ts:4,22), prepended to the ciphertext; test 'each seal uses a fresh nonce' (server/seal.test.ts:26). Better Auth uses managedNonce(xchacha20poly1305), a random 192-bit nonce per message (crypto/index.mjs:33). Volumes are small (WhatsApp jobs and creds rows) so random-nonce collision bounds for GCM are far away. pgBackRest uses a random PKCS5 salt per file (cipherBlock.c 'cryptoRandomBytes(destination, PKCS5_SALT_LEN)') from which the IV is derived. |
| V11.3.5 | L3 | pass | No app code combines a cipher with a separate MAC: all app encryption is AEAD (server/seal.ts AES-256-GCM, Better Auth XChaCha20-Poly1305). The one library that does, libsignal in Baileys, is encrypt-then-MAC: encrypt at node_modules/libsignal/src/session_cipher.js:93, MAC over the ciphertext at :100, verifyMAC at :250 before decrypt at :251. restic is AES-CTR then Poly1305 over the ciphertext (restic references: 'integrity of the encrypted data is secured by a Poly1305-AES message authentication code'). pgBackRest has no MAC (graded under V11.3.3). |
| V11.4.1 | L1 | pass | Re-checked against the tree on 21 October 2026. Hashes in cryptographic use are SHA-256 family: HMAC-SHA-256 (server/signed-link.ts:63, server/email-log.ts:30,37, worker/whatsapp/auth-state.ts:27,30), SHA-256 integrity (worker/evidence-common.ts:74-87, server/api/upload.server.ts:165, :361), SHA-256 CSP hashes (server/csp.server.ts:23), SigV4 HMAC-SHA-256 through aws4fetch. HMAC-SHA1 appears only inside RFC 6238 TOTP (server/totp-step.ts:11 and the test helper e2e/staff-session.ts:32), which authenticator apps require and which does not rely on collision resistance. md5 appears only in a test fixture (server/health.test.ts:315, :340) and as a row fingerprint in the restore drill (infra/restore-test.sh:107-116); neither is a security decision. |
| V11.4.2 | L2 | na | No passwords exist: Better Auth emailAndPassword is not configured (server/auth.server.ts:323-445) and OPEN_AUTH_PATHS routes no password endpoint (server/auth.server.ts:81-97, default closed, others 404 ENDPOINT_DISABLED at :400-405). Dev DB read only: psql -Atc "select count(\*) from accounts where password is not null" -&gt; '0'. Sign-in codes are one-time codes governed by ASVS V6 (6 digits, 300 s, 3 attempts, server/account-otp.server.ts:33,254-270). |
| V11.4.3 | L2 | pass | Integrity and authentication hashes are &gt;= 256 bits: evidence sha256 hex (db/schema/reports.ts:223, check on policy_briefs '^[0-9a-f]{64}$' db/schema/demands.ts:361), mismatch rejects (worker/evidence-common.ts:87-88 'sha256_mismatch'); public file names sha256(evidenceId\|\|bytes) (worker/evidence-publish.ts:72); signed-link tag full 32-byte HMAC-SHA-256 compared in full (server/signed-link.ts:63,91-93); CSP script/style hashes sha256 (server/csp.server.ts:23); SigV4 HMAC-SHA-256. The 64-bit truncation app/features/campus/settings.server.ts:221 is the logo cache file name (logoFile '&lt;hash&gt;.webp' :111), chosen by the tenant admin for their own logo, not an integrity control. |
| V11.4.4 | L2 | pass | check_backup_passes in infra/lib.sh refuses PGBACKREST_REPO1_CIPHER_PASS or RESTIC_PASSWORD under 40 characters, called by infra/deploy.sh; test infra/lib.test.ts 'refuses short backup passphrases'; infra/env.example comments 'openssl rand -base64 48'; infra/README.md First Setup paragraph. |

Math.random only for send jitter (worker/whatsapp-send.ts:43-46) and k6 page choice (infra/load-test.js:73). Sign-in codes (6 digits) and backup codes (10 alphanumerics) are V6 lookup and out-of-band secrets with their own entropy rules (V6.5.4). |

Passkeys are not built (server/auth.server.ts:675 TODO(P5)). |
| V11.6.2 | L3 | na | No application-configured key exchange: public TLS terminates at Cloudflare and reaches the origin through cloudflared (docs/architecture.md:842); Caddy runs 'auto_https off' (infra/Caddyfile:4) inside the tunnel; outbound fetch to Brevo, SES, R2, Turnstile uses Bun/BoringSSL defaults with no cipher or group overrides (no tls options in server/\*.ts). Fix or recommendation: Beyond target, recommendation: hardware or hosting choice, not code; record in docs/decisions.md as rejected for Phase 1 (cost, campus hardware) with the trigger 'move to a confidential-computing VM if the host becomes shared or third-party managed'. |
| V11.7.2 | L3 | gap | **Gap, beyond target (Low):** Reporter contact data is stored and processed in plaintext in Postgres: report_contacts.phone is a text column (db/schema/reports.ts), and the name, email and phone on users and the report text are plaintext too. (Correction from verification: report_contacts holds only phone and method.) Evidence: Minimisation in processing: job payloads carry IDs only or are sealed (WhatsApp code jobs AES-GCM, server/whatsapp.server.ts:15, worker/whatsapp-send.ts:27-40); no live code or address in pgboss tables (docs/architecture.md:601); identifiers stored as HMAC (server/email-log.ts:29-38, verification identifiers hashed at server/auth.server.ts:562-569 storeIdentifier); TOTP secret decrypted only during verify (server/auth.server.ts:465-468 in claimTotpStep, and inside Better Auth's verify endpoint); WhatsApp session rows sealed (worker/whatsapp/auth-state.ts:46). Fix or recommendation: Recommendation only (beyond the PLAN L2 target and the zero-budget single host): if a trigger appears (more than one host, regulator demand), move HMAC and seal operations behind a KMS or a small signing service with an HSM-backed key; until then record it as accepted in docs/decisions.md. CVSS 4.0 rationale AV:L/AC:L/AT:P/PR:H/UI:N/VC:H/VI:L/VA:N rated Low because it needs host or container compromise first. |
| V13.3.4 | L3 | gap | **Gap, beyond target (Low):** Secrets are not configured to expire: role passwords have no VALID UNTIL (infra/initdb.sql:15-18), no check flags a LACAK_LINK_KEYS signing key older than its documented one year, and rotation depends on a manual handover step. Evidence: Rotation is documented (docs/architecture.md:863 Secrets row, 'rotation per key type at handover'; docs/decisions.md:50 Secrets management) and supported by versioned keyrings (BETTER_AUTH_SECRETS, LACAK_LINK_KEYS); docs/architecture.md:827 Expiry register reminders row (manual). What it still tells an outsider is which revision runs, so whether a patch is deployed. Recorded as an accepted L3 deviation. Evidence: server/app.ts:325-329 `/healthz` returns `{ status: "ok", sha: options.sha ?? "dev" }`; probe on 5199 `/healthz` -&gt; '{"status":"ok","sha":"dev"}' (production image sets GIT_SHA, infra/Dockerfile:60,70). The Bun origin sends no Server or X-Powered-By header (probe headers on /healthz and /logo.svg). Not probed through Caddy, which by default adds 'Server: Caddy' without a version (infra/Caddyfile has no `header -Server`); Cloudflare replaces the Server header at the edge (not verified here). Fix or recommendation: Recommendation: in infra/Caddyfile change `handle /assets/*` to a matcher `@assets path_regexp ^/assets/[^/]+\.(js\|css\|woff2\|svg\|webp\|avif)$` with `handle /assets/* { respond 404 }` after it; in server/app.ts staticFrom skip paths whose extension is not in the same list (plus favicon.svg, logo.svg); fix docs/architecture.md:835 to say .webp only. CVSS 4.0 rationale AV:N/AC:L/AT:P/PR:N/UI:N/VC:L/VI:N/VA:N: needs a build that emits a sensitive file first. |

| ID | Level | Status | Evidence or Gap |
|---|---|---|---|
| V14.1.1 | L2 | pass | Fixed 2026-10-21 (batch data-logging-privacy): docs/architecture.md 'Data Classification' lists every sensitive field by level (Secret, Restricted, Confidential, Pseudonymous, Public), names the signed-link token (server/signed-link.ts:66 signLink, base64url purpose\|orgId\|subjectId\|userId\|expiry) as encoded-only readable data classed Confidential, and cites UU PDP Pasal 16(2), 22, 34, 53 from PLAN §11; linked from the Security Controls Data minimization row. |
| V14.1.2 | L2 | pass | Fixed 2026-10-21: second table of docs/architecture.md 'Data Classification' gives each level its encryption in transit and at rest, integrity, access, retention, logging rule, who may read logs, and privacy technique, linking Key Management, Starting Values and the Log Inventory. |
| V14.2.1 | L1 | pass | Re-checked against the tree on 21 October 2026. No session token or API key goes in a URL: the session is the host-only cookie `__Host-sb.session_token` (V3.3.1), /api/v1 has no key, tus metadata goes in headers, the sign-in code is typed. The one URL credential is the signed capability ?t= on /lacak, /isu/&lt;code&gt;/berhenti, /tuntutan/&lt;code&gt;/berhenti and /isu/&lt;code&gt;/survei: purpose, campus and subject bound, 30-day expiry, read-only or unfollow only (server/signed-link.ts). Mitigations: Referrer-Policy no-referrer on those pages (app/routes/account/track.tsx:24, app/routes/issues/unfollow.tsx:19, app/routes/demands/unfollow.tsx:22), private no-store for private paths (server/middleware/cache-headers.ts:21 cachePolicy), the Caddy log strips the query (infra/Caddyfile:24), the app request log writes the masked path without query (server/log.ts:54 logPath), and error reports drop query strings (server/scrub.ts QUERY_RE). |
| V14.2.2 | L2 | pass | Re-checked against the tree on 21 October 2026. One place decides caching (server/middleware/cache-headers.ts:21 cachePolicy): status 400 or above gives no-store; a write, a session cookie, Set-Cookie or a private path gives 'private, no-store' (:54) and drops Cloudflare-CDN-Cache-Control. It is applied to every document response (server/app.ts:242 withCacheHeaders, :391). The Cloudflare Cache Rule respects origin headers and bypasses on the session cookie (infra/README.md Cloudflare Checklist). In-process caches hold organizations by host, the region tree and trustedOrigins, none personal; /healthz/jobs detail is behind HEALTH_TOKEN. Caddy does not cache. Tests: server/middleware/cache-headers.test.ts, server/app.test.ts "a session cookie makes it private", "errors are no-store". |
| V14.2.3 | L2 | pass | No third-party tracker runs: analytics is self-hosted Umami, GlitchTip is self-hosted (docs/architecture.md:856-862). Caddy drops Cookie on both Umami paths (infra/Caddyfile:87,97), and every other /stats/\* path is 404. scrubTrackerPayload drops private-path views and clears private referrers (shared/private-paths.ts:29-49, and a url or referrer that does not parse is treated as private; shared/private-paths.test.ts 38 pass, 0 fail). Error events are built from an allow-list with no user, request or extra, and strings scrubbed of email, phone, IP and query (server/error-report.ts; server/error-report.test.ts 'no personal data reaches the payload' and 'removes emails, phones and addresses', 25 pass). Processors that must get personal data get only what they need: Brevo or SES get the recipient and the report title, no story (server/emails/render.ts:223). Turnstile siteverify gets remoteip (server/turnstile.server.ts:28). Meta WhatsApp gets the number for codes. The cross-border basis for these processors is an open legal item (PLAN.md §11 item 3, P22), owned elsewhere. |

| V14.2.7 | L3 | decision | **Decision:** Retention for account, report and complaint data is an open user decision (PLAN §14 item 29). Until it is decided, users, reports.story, report_contacts of rejected reports and complaints are kept with no limit. Evidence: Automatic retention exists for what has been decided: worker/maintenance.ts:26-33 (rejected originals 90 days; email_log, whatsapp_log and IP addresses 30 days; invitations 30 days after expiry), sessions' ip_address and user_agent cleared after 30 days (PRIV-08), rating client hashes cleared after 30 days, orphan sweep. maintenance-daily runs on cron 0 4 \* \* \*. Backups: pgBackRest repo1-retention-full=3, restic forget, Umami prune at 13 months, GlitchTip 30 days. Since the fix pass, a person's account can be exported and deleted on request with db/subject.ts (`bun run db:subject export\|delete`, CMP-04, CMP-06). Not decided: retention periods for account data (name, email, WhatsApp), reports and issues, and content complaints (PLAN §14 item 29 open, /privasi says 'Belum ditetapkan'). Next step: Decision for the team (PLAN §14 item 29), listed as open. Once it is decided, the backend adds the periods to the maintenance-daily handler with TDD (e.g. delete report_contacts and story text of rejected reports after the decided period, and anonymise users closed for N days). It should also add a db/jobs-cli.ts command for the DPO's global account deletion in runbook step 4, so the step is audited and repeatable. |
| V14.2.8 | L3 | decision | **Decision:** Originals keep EXIF and GPS with no explicit consent or notice at submission. Keeping them is a deliberate product and legal choice. Evidence: Beyond target (L3). Every public or derived copy has its metadata removed. Images are re-encoded by sharp (worker/evidence-common.ts:56, 'Re-encode strips all metadata'). Tests: worker/evidence.test.ts 'strips EXIF and GPS, applies orientation' (it checks that the fixture holds GPS IFD 0x8825 and that the output has meta.exif undefined, 23 pass) and worker/media.test.ts 'output carries no metadata' and 'transcode caps short side at 720 and strips tags'. Video uses ffmpeg '-map_metadata -1 -map_chapters -1 -sn -dn' (worker/video.ts:86-91). PDFs are rasterized and never published (evidence.test.ts 'PDF pages become previews, never a PDF'). Policy briefs go through qpdf --remove-info --remove-metadata (worker/pdf.ts:48, evidence-brief.test.ts 'the public copy is the stripped one'). Logos are re-encoded without metadata (architecture.md:830). The original is kept with its metadata, by design, as evidence on the GlobaLeaks pattern (PLAN.md §10.5 Unggahan, §11 item 5). The submit consent texts (messages/id.json:304-310 consent\_\*) do not tell the reporter that the original, including GPS, is stored. /privasi says only 'Berkas aslinya disimpan privat' (id.json:681) and that metadata is removed from processed copies (id.json:743). Next step: Decision with the legal review in PLAN §14 item 12: either (a) state in consent\_\* or the evidence step, and in /privasi, that the original file, including location metadata, is kept privately as evidence for N days (a frontend text change, handoff), or (b) strip GPS from the original at ingest and keep only a sha256 of the untouched file. Option (b) would be a worker change with a test, but it weakens the evidence value PLAN §11 item 5 chose. |
| V14.3.1 | L1 | pass | Sign-out ends the server session and passes Better Auth's cookie-clearing Set-Cookie headers on a 303 (app/features/report/sign-out.server.ts:22-35; app/features/report/sign-out.test.ts 3 pass). The draft is in sessionStorage, which is per tab and gone when the tab closes (app/features/report/draft-store.ts:99-105), and files are held in memory only (draft-store.ts:89-96). Staff removal deletes sessions on every host (architecture.md:828). Authenticated pages are 'private, no-store' (curl '/masuk' -&gt; 'Cache-Control: private, no-store'), so the bfcache and the HTTP cache keep no copy. grep for 'Clear-Site-Data' over app, server, shared and worker found nothing. |
| V14.3.2 | L2 | pass | Every sensitive response is 'private, no-store': private paths, session-cookie requests, writes and Set-Cookie responses (server/middleware/cache-headers.ts:22-35, 52-55). Tests: cache-headers.test.ts 10 pass, and server/app.test.ts 'a session cookie makes it private' (expects 'private, no-store' at line 159) and 'report form is private' (line 174). curl against the pre-existing server on 127.0.0.1:5199, Host itb.localhost: '/lacak?t=bogus' 'Cache-Control: private, no-store'. '/lacak.data?t=bogus' gives the same: the route header covers React Router data requests, which matters because isPrivatePath('/lacak.data') alone would not match. '/masuk' and '/masuk.data' 'private, no-store'. '/kelola.data' '403' 'no-store'. The public '/isu' gives 'public, max-age=0, no-transform', as designed. |
| V14.3.3 | L2 | frontend | **Frontend (Low):** Browser storage holds sensitive report content (story text, location, relation), not only session tokens. The control lives in app/ UI (teammate). Evidence: Cookies hold only the session token: host-only \_\_Host-sb.session_token, HttpOnly, Secure, SameSite=Lax (PLAN.md §10.5 Sesi, server/auth.server.ts). The report draft goes into sessionStorage key 'sm.report-draft' (app/features/report/draft-store.ts:79, 102, 107-112). It holds DetailsAnswer.story and title, location codes and label, relation and other-consent, impact, evidence names and sizes, and contact method (draft-store.ts:4-56). The story is free text that may hold specific personal data (PLAN §11 item 3). PLAN.md:89 (mockup critique item 4) chose sessionStorage over localStorage on purpose, with clearing on sign-out and on submit (root.tsx:160, review.tsx:360). Files are kept in memory only (draft-store.ts:89-96). Handoff: Frontend handoff (app/features/report/draft-store.ts): either keep the draft in memory only and accept losing it on reload, or keep it in sessionStorage with a TTL (e.g. drop a draft older than 2 h on read) and record the accepted risk in docs/decisions.md. Backend support: the optional Clear-Site-Data on sign-out (V14.3.1). CVSS 4.0 Low (AV:P/AC:L/AT:P/PR:N/UI:P/VC:L): needs physical access to an unlocked shared device with the same tab still open. |

| V15.4.2 | L3 | gap | **Gap, beyond target (Low):** Found by code reading, not reproduced: (1) an upload checks the report state once before streaming (server/api/upload.server.ts:634 and :686 `checkOpen`, defined at :285, refuses forwarded or rejected) in a different transaction from `commit` (:296), whose UPDATE checks only evidence.status; streaming can take minutes in between. forwardReport locks the report FOR UPDATE and copies awaiting rows to the other campus but never updates the source evidence rows (app/features/report/forward.server.ts), so an upload that started before a forward can still commit the file at the first campus. The test "a forwarded or rejected report takes no upload" sets the status before the upload starts, so the in-flight case is untested. (2) The per-tenant 24 h byte cap is read before streaming, so concurrent uploads can overshoot it by up to the 16 upload slots times the per-file limit. Evidence: Most check-then-act paths are atomic: upload commit is a CAS on evidence.status (server/api/upload.server.ts:301-318); issue ratings gate and insert in one INSERT ... SELECT ... WHERE EXISTS under an advisory lock (app/features/issues/ratings.server.ts:112, docs/decisions.md:67); moderation uses SELECT ... FOR UPDATE or version CAS (merge.server.ts:61, complaints.server.ts:619, forward.server.ts:64); tests: complaints.test.ts:426 'two admins at once, one decision', referrals.test.ts:304, members.test.ts:379 'two admins removing each other leave one admin'. Fix or recommendation: In commit(), take `SELECT status FROM reports WHERE id = evidence.report_id FOR SHARE` in the same transaction before the UPDATE (blocks behind forward's FOR UPDATE, then sees the new status), add `AND NOT EXISTS (reports with status in UPLOAD_CLOSED)` to the CAS, and return 409 report_closed when it loses; test: start an upload, run forwardReport, finish the upload, expect no received evidence at campus A. Accept the cap overshoot as documented (bounded) or re-check the cap inside commit. CVSS 4.0 rationale: needs a race with an admin action by the reporter, AV:N/AC:H/AT:P/PR:L/UI:N/VC:L/VI:L/VA:N. |
| V15.4.3 | L3 | pass | In-process upload locks are private to the createUploadHandler closure (server/api/upload.server.ts:505-555): a newcomer aborts the holder and waits at most TAKEOVER_MS, release only deletes its own entry (locks.get(id) === mine), so no outside code can modify them and no wait is unbounded; DB locks are transaction-scoped only (pg_advisory_xact_lock everywhere, no session advisory locks: grep pg_advisory in server/ worker/ app/features) and every pool sets lock_timeout 3000-5000 ms (db/client.server.ts:57-90,103), so waits end; withTenant retries are capped at 3 attempts and 10 s (docs/architecture.md:816); pg-boss expireInSeconds above handler timeout prevents overlapping retries (docs/architecture.md:814). |
| V15.4.4 | L3 | pass | Separate bounded pools per class of work: page vs upload load-shed slots (server/app.ts:203-214), 2 search slots (app/features/issues/issues.server.ts:45), per-IP upload slots (server/api/upload.server.ts:510-524), per-profile DB pools with a 2 s acquire timeout (db/client.server.ts:57-111), separate worker containers per queue group with their own CPU and memory limits (infra/compose.yaml:153-157, 214-220, 250-254, cpus 1.5 for worker-media), OTP reserve within the email quota so notifications cannot starve sign-in codes (docs/architecture.md:786, server/email-log.ts:172-200), per-tenant upload cap so one tenant cannot exhaust shared disk (docs/architecture.md:791). |

Hardening beyond L2: owner sb_migrator still holds TRUNCATE (psql `t`) and the trigger is row-level only. A `BEFORE TRUNCATE ... FOR EACH STATEMENT` trigger, or a per-row HMAC chain with AUDIT_HMAC_KEY, would make privileged tampering detectable. No hash chain exists today (db/schema/audit.ts has no prev hash column). |

Remaining (user decision): the server's bucket key can delete, so root can still erase the copy; needs a B2 key without deleteFiles or Object Lock with prune off the server (decisions.md Log shipping). Fix or recommendation: User decision: B2 application key without deleteFiles or Object Lock, prune from an operator machine. |

| V16.5.4 | L3 | gap | **Gap, beyond target (Low):** Narrowed. Error details are no longer lost, but the handler deliberately fails fast: the process drains and exits 1 and Docker restarts it, so the second half of the requirement (an error does not take down the process) is not met. Fail-fast after an unknown error was chosen on purpose because process state may be corrupt. Evidence: Last-resort handlers now exist: server/index.ts:100-107 and worker/index.ts:459-467 register `onFatal` (server/shutdown.ts:75) for `uncaughtException` and `unhandledRejection`; it writes one scrubbed `fatal` record (errorRecord, server/log.ts:112-131), drains, flushes the error reporter and exits 1, with a hard exit after the grace period. Tests: server/shutdown.test.ts "an unhandled error is logged scrubbed, then stops once", "a failed shutdown exits 1 after the drain". Containers restart with `restart: unless-stopped`. Request-scope handlers: Hono onError (server/app.ts:291-299) and React Router handleError. Fix or recommendation: Recommendation only: keep fail-fast, or record it as an accepted deviation in docs/decisions.md. |

## OWASP Top 10 2025

Each category has a summary row (A01 to A10) and one row per group of mapped CWEs from the category file. Categories: A01 Broken Access Control; A02 Security Misconfiguration; A03 Software Supply Chain Failures; A04 Cryptographic Failures; A05 Injection; A06 Insecure Design; A07 Authentication Failures; A08 Software or Data Integrity Failures; A09 Security Logging and Alerting Failures; A10 Mishandling of Exceptional Conditions.

## OWASP API Security Top 10 2023

Applied to `/api/v1`, the tus upload API and the Better Auth JSON API. The public data API contract is in [Public Data API](architecture.md#public-data-api).

| ID | Status | Evidence or Gap |
|---|---|---|
| API1 Broken Object Level Authorization | pass | /api/v1: tenant from Host only (server/api/public-data.ts:222-233), every read goes through the page's \*.server.ts function under withTenant with FORCE RLS (44 FORCE ROW LEVEL SECURITY statements in db/migrations, withTenant sets app.org_id at db/tenant.server.ts:72); codes are normalized (public-data.ts:286-288, 379-381) and unpublished, draft, hidden and foreign codes answer 404, taken-down 410 (public-data.ts:291-299, 384). Live run 2026-10-21 14:41-14:46 WIB: production build (NODE_ENV=production bun server/index.ts) on 127.0.0.1:5199 against scratch copy sadar_bersuara_api2023_r2 (TEMPLATE sadar_bersuara, dropped afterwards), EMAIL_PROVIDER=brevo with a dummy key (no mail sent), Host itb.localhost unless noted: GET /api/v1/isu/ISU-UGM-26-0001 on itb host -&gt; 404 not_found while the same code on ugm.localhost -&gt; 200; ISU-ITB-26-0037 (unpublished) -&gt; 404; ISU-ITB-26-0010 -&gt; 410 taken_down; TUN-ITB-26-0007 (draft) -&gt; 404; TUN-UGM-26-0001 on itb -&gt; 404; apex Host localhost and unknown Host evil.localhost -&gt; 404. tus upload: owner check after the RLS-scoped row read, missing and foreign answer the same 404 (server/api/upload.server.ts:615-619 POST, 666-667 HEAD, 734-737 PATCH; findUpload runs withTenant role reporter at 231-271). Live with two real reporter sessions minted on the scratch DB: owner HEAD on evidence 01a15c92-... -&gt; `200 Upload-Offset: 0 Upload-Length: 245760`; another itb reporter HEAD -&gt; 404 and PATCH -&gt; `{"error":"not_found"} [404]`; owner HEAD of the ugm evidence id 01a15c93-... on the itb host -&gt; 404. React Router: /aduan/:code loads only the caller's complaint (app/routes/account/complaint.tsx:48-55 getOwnComplaint with session.user.id, else 404); /undangan/:id shows the invitee email only to its owner (app/routes/account/invitation.tsx:41-63); /kelola/bukti/:id/\* and /kelola/bukti/:id/asli run requireAdmin then tenant-scoped reads (app/routes/manage/evidence.ts:13-15, evidence-original.ts:16-21); /tuntutan/:code/dokumen/:file serves only a clean, published brief of a public tuntutan (app/features/demands/demands.server.ts:747-783). IDs are UUIDs (generateId uuid, server/auth.server.ts:375; UUID checks upload.server.ts:53,606). Tests: db/tenant.test.ts "every table the app roles reach has FORCE RLS or is allowlisted", "reads only the current org", "cannot update or delete another org's rows", "the owner is subject to FORCE"; server/api/public-data.test.ts "unpublished and other tenants' issues are 404", "drafts and other tenants are 404, the list skips drafts"; server/api/upload.test.ts "another user's evidence is 404", "another tenant cannot see the row", "only the signed-in owner sees the upload"; app/routes/manage/campus-routes.test.ts "an admin of campus A is refused on campus B". Run: `bun test server/api/public-data.test.ts server/api/upload.test.ts server/middleware/rate-limit.test.ts server/client-ip.test.ts server/request-context.test.ts server/api/csv.test.ts` -&gt; `98 pass 0 fail`; `bun test server/auth.test.ts app/routes/manage/campus-routes.test.ts db/tenant.test.ts server/turnstile.test.ts server/email.test.ts` -&gt; `114 pass 0 fail`. |
| API2 Broken Authentication | gap | **Gap (Medium):** Identifier-first /api/auth/account/lookup and /api/auth/account/check-new tell an unauthenticated caller whether an email or WhatsApp number belongs to an account (and whether it sits unproven on one), guarded only by the per-IP 20/60 s limiter, no captcha; an attacker rotating addresses can test lists of emails or numbers for membership of a citizen-complaint platform. 0xa2 lists an API as vulnerable when it 'Permits credential stuffing where the attacker uses brute force with a list of valid usernames' (lookup and check-new build that list) and asks that anti-brute-force mechanisms on authentication endpoints 'be stricter than the regular rate limiting mechanisms' (lookup and check-new get the same CGNAT_LIMIT as verify-code and no captcha). Recorded as accepted exposure (docs/architecture.md:291 Rate limiter row, docs/decisions.md:301 ADR-08 Abuse Controls, docs/architecture.md:1444 Open Questions item 9(c)). Evidence: Flows: passwordless only, Better Auth with a default-closed route allowlist (server/auth.server.ts:96-112 OPEN_AUTH_PATHS, the before hook at :610-616 answers 404 ENDPOINT_DISABLED for every other path). Live run 2026-10-21 14:41-14:46 WIB: production build (NODE_ENV=production bun server/index.ts) on 127.0.0.1:5199 against scratch copy sadar_bersuara_api2023_r2 (TEMPLATE sadar_bersuara, dropped afterwards), EMAIL_PROVIDER=brevo with a dummy key (no mail sent), Host itb.localhost unless noted: POST /api/auth/{sign-up/email, sign-in/email, update-user, change-email, delete-user, revoke-sessions, organization/create, organization/update-member-role, organization/invite-member, two-factor/disable, two-factor/get-totp-uri, reset-password, request-password-reset} -&gt; each `{"code":"ENDPOINT_DISABLED","message":"Not found"} [404]`; list-sessions and admin/set-role -&gt; 404. Brute force: 21st POST /api/auth/account/lookup from X-Client-IP 203.0.113.60 within 60 s -&gt; `200 x20, 429 429` with `X-Retry-After: 60`, a second IP still 200 (CGNAT_LIMIT 20/60 s on lookup, check-new, send-code, verify-code: server/auth.server.ts:136, :547-551). Codes: 6 digits, SHA-256 stored, 300 s, 3 attempts, race-safe consume (server/account-otp.server.ts:37, :258-274), Turnstile on every send (auth.server.ts:630-640), per address and per number and client 60 s spacing and 8 per 24 h, plus 20 per 24 h from all clients (docs/architecture.md:291 Rate limiter row; guess budget in V6.6.3). TOTP: replay claimed per step (claimTotpStep auth.server.ts:454-496), upstream per-IP rule `/two-factor/*` 3 per 10 s on direct HTTP calls only (node_modules/better-auth/dist/plugins/two-factor/index.mjs:338-344; the staff pages call in-process, V6.1.3) and per-account lockout 10 failures for 900 s on by default (verify-two-factor.mjs:117-122, columns db/schema/auth.ts:122-125). trustDevice refused (auth.server.ts:618-627). Re-authentication: TOTP step-up within 300 s before member, settings and campus changes (app/features/moderation/step-up.server.ts:32-40, used by team-actions.server.ts:58, settings-actions.server.ts:52, campus-actions.server.ts:37); email change closed (404 above). Sessions: opaque DB sessions, host-only `__Host-sb.session_token` HttpOnly SameSite=Lax Secure in production (server/auth.server.ts:581-583; test server/auth.test.ts "production cookies carry the \_\_Host- prefix"). Tokens in URLs: /lacak?t= is an HMAC link, 30-day expiry, `Referrer-Policy: no-referrer` (app/routes/account/track.tsx:24,46), query stripped from the Caddy log (infra/Caddyfile:21). JWT alg/expiry and weak-password checks: not applicable (no JWT, no passwords). Tests: server/auth.test.ts "no other fields, no upstream OTP routes", "unused upstream routes are closed", "three wrong codes end the code; a code works once", "a TOTP code works once; a backup code signs in", "a flood from one address is throttled", "one address waits 60 s and gets 8 codes a day". Enumeration: live POST /account/lookup for an unknown address -&gt; `{"found":false} [200]`; for a known proven identifier the endpoint returns found:true plus channel masks (server/account-otp.server.ts:300-325), and check-new returns taken/only (account-otp.server.ts:327-342), with no Turnstile on either. Fix or recommendation: CVSS 4.0 AV:N/AC:L/AT:N/PR:N/UI:N/VC:L/VI:N/VA:N/SC:N/SI:N/SA:N (unauthenticated network read of account existence, no integrity or availability impact). Backend options, user to choose since it is an accepted exposure: (a) put Turnstile in front of lookup and check-new as on send-code (verifyCaptcha in the auth before-hook for ACCOUNT_PATHS.lookup and checkNew, x-captcha-response header; TDD in server/auth.test.ts: lookup without a token -&gt; 403 CAPTCHA_FAILED), which keeps the identifier-first UX (frontend handoff: SignIn must pass the Turnstile token on these two calls); or (b) make lookup answer the same shape for every identifier and move the 'not found' outcome behind send-code, which already sits behind Turnstile. Either way lower CGNAT_LIMIT for lookup only, and update docs/decisions.md:287. |

Tests: worker/media.test.ts "a playlist pretending to be MP4 reads no network"; worker/evidence.test.ts "a playlist named MP4 needs review". |

API documentation: /api/v1 contract with every endpoint, field, error code, rate limit, CORS, cache and licence rule in docs/architecture.md Public Data API (lines 621-671); tus contract with every status in docs/architecture.md Resumable Upload Contract (673-708); auth endpoints and body allowlists in docs/architecture.md:288-290; survey and right-of-reply route contract at docs/architecture.md:709-733; machine-readable index at GET /api/v1 (`endpoints`) and GET /api/v1/data (`meta.datasets` with columns and CSV URLs, public-data.ts:237-251, 438-463). Versioning and retirement: URL major versions, additive changes only, Deprecation (RFC 9745) and Sunset (RFC 8594) headers for a retiring version, /api/v2 on a breaking change (docs/decisions.md:34); internal endpoints unversioned with one-release payload compatibility. Only one version (v1) exists. Data flows: third-party processors (Brevo, Amazon SES, Cloudflare incl. R2, Backblaze B2, Meta WhatsApp) listed with the transfer question in PLAN.md:1101 and :1126 and docs/decisions.md:101; job payloads carry IDs only (docs/architecture.md:841). Non-production data: dev and CI use seeds (db/seed/sample.ts), restore drills run on scratch clusters. Recommendation beyond the vulnerability criteria: the contract is hand-written with no OpenAPI document and no CI check that docs/architecture.md lists the same 14 DATASET_IDS (server/api/datasets.ts) and error codes as the code; a test comparing the docs table to DATASET_IDS would catch drift cheaply. |

## Compliance

Technical controls with evidence first, then obligations that only the organisation can meet. The legal frame is PLAN §11; article numbers are those cited there or fetched from pasal.id (quoted with the URL in the citing row or in docs/runbook.md). Organisational obligations stay open until the user decides; none is marked done.
